- **ahr-citation-and-style**: Use when formatting citations and notes for an American Historical Review (AHR) manuscript. Gets the note apparatus right; it does not write the prose or argument. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/American-Historical-Review-Skills/skills/ahr-citation-and-style) · ★1.1k)
- **ahr-historiography-positioning**: Use when positioning an American Historical Review (AHR) manuscript within the historiography so it reads as an intervention, not a report. Stakes the contribution against prior work; it does not write the literature survey. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/American-Historical-Review-Skills/skills/ahr-historiography-positioning) · ★1.1k)
- **ahr-interpretation-and-method**: Use when choosing and defending an interpretive approach for an American Historical Review (AHR) manuscript — social, cultural, intellectual, political, economic, transnational, global, microhistory, or the history of knowledge. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/American-Historical-Review-Skills/skills/ahr-interpretation-and-method) · ★1.1k)
- **ahr-review-process**: Use to understand how The American Historical Review (AHR) evaluates a manuscript — double-blind anonymous review by at least six scholars, a six-to-eight-month timeline, an 8-10% acceptance rate, and the large commissioned book/media review section. Sets expectations and shapes the article to survive review; it does not contact editors. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/American-Historical-Review-Skills/skills/ahr-review-process) · ★1.1k)
- **ahr-revision-and-response**: Use when responding to an American Historical Review (AHR) decision — a revise-and-resubmit or rejection. Structures the revision and response letter; it does not fabricate new evidence. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/American-Historical-Review-Skills/skills/ahr-revision-and-response) · ★1.1k)
- **ahr-sources-and-archives**: Use when handling primary sources and archives for an American Historical Review (AHR) manuscript — distinguishing primary from secondary, criticizing sources, citing manuscripts and collections in Chicago notes, and clearing image permissions. ([brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills](https://github.com/brycewang-stanford/Awesome-Journal-Skills/tree/HEAD/American-Historical-Review-Skills/skills/ahr-sources-and-archives) · ★1.1k)
- **ahr-structure-and-exposition**: Use when organizing an American Historical Review (AHR) article so narrative and analysis work together within the roughly 8,000-word target (notes excluded). - **ahr-submission**: Use when running the final pre-submission preflight for The American Historical Review (AHR) via ScholarOne — masked-author preparation, the roughly 8,000-word target, Chicago notes, manuscript formatting, image alt-text and permissions, and the exclusivity declaration.
